But stopping isn't easy; if I even think about eating normal foods like pizza, I get this tight, panicky feeling in my chest. Keeping my weight down is really the only thing that makes me feel in control of my life right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you fear that if you were to gain a small amount of weight, it would be impossible for you to stop gaining weight and you would become severely overweight?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. If I gain even a little bit, I feel like I'll lose control completely and my stomach will just blow up, erasing all my progress overnight. Just thinking about it gives me a tight, panicky feeling in my chest because I'm terrified of slipping back into being that sloppy, out-of-shape guy my ex-wife used to nag about.\n\nDoctor: Do you also have a strong desire to engage in excessive exercise or other compensatory behaviors, such as purging, to prevent weight gain or to compensate for any calories you consume?\n\nPatient: No, I don't purge or do any crazy workouts at all. I mostly just try to watch what I eat and keep my meals very light.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your menstrual cycle, such as skipped periods or infrequent periods, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Actually, I'm a man, so I don't have a menstrual cycle. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: Everyone keeps saying I’m way too thin and sick, but I honestly don’t see what the big deal is about my weight right now. My coworkers and the doctors at the urgent care got really worked up and insisted I come here, but I think they're just overreacting.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently at a healthy level, despite what others have told you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I honestly feel like my weight is perfectly fine and that I just look fit, maybe a little tired from my shifts at the warehouse. Everyone else, from my coworkers to the doctors, is making a mountain out of a molehill. I really don't think it's a big deal.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself checking your weight or body shape repeatedly, or do you avoid thinking about it altogether?\n\nPatient: I do check my weight, and honestly, I kind of like seeing the numbers on the scale keep dropping and thinking I can go a bit lower. I focus on staying lean because I don't want to end up like that sloppy, out-of-shape guy my ex-wife used to nag about. It's definitely not something I avoid thinking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your weight loss is due to your efforts to control it, such as through dieting or exercise, and do you believe you could stop trying to lose weight if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been deliberately eating very little—usually just coffee and some soup or a small salad—because I don't want to feel heavy or stuffed. But stopping isn't easy; if I even think about eating normal foods like pizza, I get this tight, panicky feeling in my chest. Keeping my weight down is really the only thing that makes me feel in control of my life right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you fear that if you were to gain a small amount of weight, it would be impossible for you to stop gaining weight and you would become severely overweight?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. If I gain even a little bit, I feel like I'll lose control completely and my stomach will just blow up, erasing all my progress overnight. Just thinking about it gives me a tight, panicky feeling in my chest because I'm terrified of slipping back into being that sloppy, out-of-shape guy my ex-wife used to nag about.\n\nDoctor: Do you also have a strong desire to engage in excessive exercise or other compensatory behaviors, such as purging, to prevent weight gain or to compensate for any calories you consume?\n\nPatient: No, I don't purge or do any crazy workouts at all. I mostly just try to watch what I eat and keep my meals very light.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your menstrual cycle, such as skipped periods or infrequent periods, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Actually, I'm a man, so I don't have a menstrual cycle. I think there might be a bit of a mix-up there.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your self-worth is closely tied to your weight or body shape, and that being thin is essential to your identity or sense of being a good person?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. Staying lean is really the one thing that makes me feel like I’m doing something right and staying in control of my life. If I let my weight creep back up even a little, I feel like I’ll become that sloppy, out-of-shape guy my ex-wife used to nag about.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'that sloppy, out-of-shape guy' your ex-wife used to nag about, and how that image affects your perception of yourself and your weight?"

Just thinking about it gives me a tight, panicky feeling in my chest because I'm terrified of slipping back into being that sloppy, out-of-shape guy my ex-wife used to nag about.\n\nDoctor: Do you also have a strong desire to engage in excessive exercise or other compensatory behaviors, such as purging, to prevent weight gain or to compensate for any calories you consume?\n\nPatient: No, I don't purge or do any crazy workouts at all. I mostly just try to watch what I eat and keep my meals very light.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your menstrual cycle, such as skipped periods or infrequent periods, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Actually, I'm a man, so I don't have a menstrual cycle. I think there might be a bit of a mix-up there.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your self-worth is closely tied to your weight or body shape, and that being thin is essential to your identity or sense of being a good person?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. Staying lean is really the one thing that makes me feel like I’m doing something right and staying in control of my life. If I let my weight creep back up even a little, I feel like I’ll become that sloppy, out-of-shape guy my ex-wife used to nag about.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'that sloppy, out-of-shape guy' your ex-wife used to nag about, and how that image affects your perception of yourself and your weight?\n\nPatient: During my marriage, my ex always nagged me about being out of shape, which made me feel like a complete failure. After the divorce and my back injury, staying lean became the one area where I actually feel successful and in control of my life. If I let my weight creep back up, I feel like I'm slipping right back into being that sloppy guy, and it would feel like a total personal failure.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your distorted body image or excessive weight loss is primarily driven by a desire to achieve a certain body ideal, or is it more about avoiding the negative feelings associated with being overweight, such as feeling like a failure?"
  }

Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.
